At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIs that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other unusual sights, sounds, or sensations that others around you don’t seem to notice, outside of the voices you hear at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed anything else, really… no unusual sights or feelings that I can think of.\n\nIt’s mainly just that one thing at work—the voices through the monitor speakers during the quiet stretches. I know I’ve said that already, but that’s what scared me enough to come in. They’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” even when the channels are muted and no one else hears it. I’m not trying to make too much of it, but… it does bother me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts are being placed in your mind, or that someone else can hear your thoughts or is controlling them?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I haven’t felt like anyone is putting thoughts in my head or controlling them.\n\nAnd I don’t think anyone can hear my thoughts. That sounds… more extreme than what I’ve been dealing with, I guess. I’m answering that right, aren’t I? Like, I’d know if that was happening?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I… not like special abilities, no. And I don’t think anyone is following me exactly.

But there is something I get kind of stuck on at work. I keep replaying every missed camera angle or any call I made a little late, like it’s proof I’m careless… dangerous, even. Other people tell me I did what I could or that it wasn’t that serious, but it doesn’t really land.

I don’t think I’ve thought someone was lying to me, exactly. It’s more about work… and I know I’ve kind of said this already, sorry.

I don’t think I’ve had a special role or anything like that. And I don’t want to overstate it.

But… yes, I guess there are times I get very convinced I’ve done something wrong at work. I’ll keep going over a missed camera angle or a call I made too late, and it starts to feel like clear proof that I’m careless, like I’m dangerous to have there. And then I get this thought that if anything goes wrong on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I know that probably sounds extreme… I’m sorry. Is that what you’re asking about?

During the quiet parts I hear it through the monitor speakers, like someone saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” even when everything’s muted.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but no one else seems to hear it, and that’s what worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that someone or something is trying to communicate with you in a special way, or that events around you have a hidden meaning that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that kind of way. I mean, I can get uneasy and wonder if I’m missing something, but I haven’t felt like there are special messages meant for me, or hidden meanings in things. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIs that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other unusual sights, sounds, or sensations that others around you don’t seem to notice, outside of the voices you hear at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed anything else, really… no unusual sights or feelings that I can think of.\n\nIt’s mainly just that one thing at work—the voices through the monitor speakers during the quiet stretches. I know I’ve said that already, but that’s what scared me enough to come in. They’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” even when the channels are muted and no one else hears it. I’m not trying to make too much of it, but… it does bother me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts are being placed in your mind, or that someone else can hear your thoughts or is controlling them?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I haven’t felt like anyone is putting thoughts in my head or controlling them.\n\nAnd I don’t think anyone can hear my thoughts. That sounds… more extreme than what I’ve been dealing with, I guess. I’m answering that right, aren’t I? Like, I’d know if that was happening?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other strange sounds, voices, or sensations that other people around you didn’t seem to notice, even briefly, at any time in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s been anything else, really… not that I can remember from before.\n\nIt’s just this thing at work, and I know I keep coming back to it. During the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, I’ll hear voices coming through the monitor speakers, even when every channel is muted. They’ll say my name sometimes—like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else seems to hear anything.\n\nI’m sorry if I’m repeating myself. That’s just the part that scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time where you felt persistently sad, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy, for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days lately it feels like this heavy gray mood is already there before I even get out of bed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be driving in to my shift and just seeing the casino lights can make me tear up, even if nothing really happened. It feels silly saying that out loud, but… yes, it’s been more than a couple weeks, I think. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, or not getting as much pleasure from them as before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I mean, I still tell myself I’m just tired from work, but it’s not really the same.\n\nI used to really look forward to salsa class, and softball, and I’d get excited about building PCs or having people over for board games. Lately I just… ignore the texts. I stay home and sit there like none of it can really reach me. That counts, right? Like, that’s what you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt your thoughts were racing, where it was different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had nights where I don’t sleep well, especially with work, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually energetic from it or like I didn’t need sleep.\n\nMy thoughts can feel busy when I’m worried, but not in that… sped-up way, I don’t think. If that makes sense? I don’t think I’ve had a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others around you didn’t share, even when there was evidence against it, like believing someone was following you or that you had a special ability?\n\nPatient: I… not like special abilities, no. And I don’t think anyone is following me exactly.\n\nBut there is something I get kind of stuck on at work. I keep replaying every missed camera angle or any call I made a little late, like it’s proof I’m careless… dangerous, even. Other people tell me I did what I could or that it wasn’t that serious, but it doesn’t really land.\n\nPart of me feels like if something goes wrong on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I know that probably sounds extreme. I’m not trying to be dramatic… I just don’t know how else to explain it. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something very strongly that most people would think is not true, like thinking you are being watched by unseen forces or that you have a special role or mission that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not unseen forces or a special mission, no… I don’t think it’s like that.\n\nIt’s more… I get really convinced I’ve messed up at work, even if people tell me I didn’t. Like I’ll replay a missed camera angle or a call I made too late, and it feels like proof that I’m careless, or dangerous somehow. And if something goes wrong on my watch, part of me feels like… I deserve to be punished for it.\n\nI know that probably sounds too harsh. I’m not trying to make it into something it isn’t. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something strongly that wasn’t true, even when people gave you proof it wasn’t, like thinking someone was lying to you or that something bad was going to happen that didn’t seem real to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve thought someone was lying to me, exactly. It’s more about work… and I know I’ve kind of said this already, sorry.\n\nI’ll get really fixed on a missed camera angle or a call I made later than I should have, and it starts to feel like definite proof that I’m careless, or that I’m dangerous to have in that role. Even if someone shows me it wasn’t my fault or says I did what I could, I have a hard time believing them.\n\nAnd then I think… if something bad happens on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I know that probably sounds excessive. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something very strongly that wasn’t true and couldn’t be convinced otherwise, even when others gave you clear evidence it wasn’t real—like thinking something bad was going to happen that they didn’t believe, or that you had a special role no one else understood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had a special role or anything like that. And I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut… yes, I guess there are times I get very convinced I’ve done something wrong at work. I’ll keep going over a missed camera angle or a call I made too late, and it starts to feel like clear proof that I’m careless, like I’m dangerous to have there. People can tell me it wasn’t my fault or that I handled it okay, and I hear them, but I don’t really believe it.\n\nAnd then I get this thought that if anything goes wrong on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I know that probably sounds extreme… I’m sorry. Is that what you’re asking about?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Outside of work… not really, I don’t think. I mean, I don’t have beliefs about other people or anything unusual like that.

It mostly stays tied to work, if that makes sense. I keep replaying things I missed, like a camera angle I didn’t catch or a call I made late, and it feels like proof that I’m careless… or dangerous to have in that position. And even when people try to reassure me, part of me still thinks if something goes wrong while I’m there, I deserve to be punished for it.

I know that may not exactly answer the “outside of work” part. I’m sorry—I’m trying to be clear.

I’m not trying to make too much of it, but… it does bother me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts are being placed in your mind, or that someone else can hear your thoughts or is controlling them?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. During the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, I’ll hear voices coming through the monitor speakers, even when every channel is muted. They’ll say my name sometimes—like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else seems to hear anything.\n\nI’m sorry if I’m repeating myself. That’s just the part that scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time where you felt persistently sad, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy, for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days lately it feels like this heavy gray mood is already there before I even get out of bed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be driving in to my shift and just seeing the casino lights can make me tear up, even if nothing really happened. It feels silly saying that out loud, but… yes, it’s been more than a couple weeks, I think. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, or not getting as much pleasure from them as before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. Like, that’s what you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt your thoughts were racing, where it was different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. If that makes sense? I don’t think I’ve had a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others around you didn’t share, even when there was evidence against it, like believing someone was following you or that you had a special ability?\n\nPatient: I… not like special abilities, no. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something very strongly that wasn’t true and couldn’t be convinced otherwise, even when others gave you clear evidence it wasn’t real—like thinking something bad was going to happen that they didn’t believe, or that you had a special role no one else understood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had a special role or anything like that. And I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut… yes, I guess there are times I get very convinced I’ve done something wrong at work. I’ll keep going over a missed camera angle or a call I made too late, and it starts to feel like clear proof that I’m careless, like I’m dangerous to have there. People can tell me it wasn’t my fault or that I handled it okay, and I hear them, but I don’t really believe it.\n\nAnd then I get this thought that if anything goes wrong on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I know that probably sounds extreme… I’m sorry. Is that what you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever had beliefs or thoughts that others around you didn’t share, and that you held onto even when there was evidence that they weren’t true?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really, I don’t think. I mean, I don’t have beliefs about other people or anything unusual like that.\n\nIt mostly stays tied to work, if that makes sense. I keep replaying things I missed, like a camera angle I didn’t catch or a call I made late, and it feels like proof that I’m careless… or dangerous to have in that position. And even when people try to reassure me, part of me still thinks if something goes wrong while I’m there, I deserve to be punished for it.\n\nI know that may not exactly answer the “outside of work” part. I’m sorry—I’m trying to be clear.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever experienced any delusions or hallucinations outside of the voices you’ve heard at work—like seeing things others don’t, believing something strongly that isn’t true, or hearing voices when no one is around?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really seeing things, no. And I don’t think I hear them other places… or at least not that I’ve noticed.

The thing that scared me enough to come in is at work, during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room. I’ll hear voices through the monitor speakers saying things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” even when I’m pretty sure every channel is muted. And no one else hears it.

I know that sounds… strange. I’m not trying to make it dramatic. That’s just the part I couldn’t explain away anymore.

During the quiet parts I hear it through the monitor speakers, like someone saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” even when everything’s muted.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but no one else seems to hear it, and that’s what worries me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that someone or something is trying to communicate with you in a special way, or that events around you have a hidden meaning that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in that kind of way. At least, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIs that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other unusual sights, sounds, or sensations that others around you don’t seem to notice, outside of the voices you hear at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed anything else, really… no unusual sights or feelings that I can think of.\n\nIt’s mainly just that one thing at work—the voices through the monitor speakers during the quiet stretches. I know I’ve said that already, but that’s what scared me enough to come in. I’m not trying to make too much of it, but… it does bother me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that thoughts are being placed in your mind, or that someone else can hear your thoughts or is controlling them?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I haven’t felt like anyone is putting thoughts in my head or controlling them.\n\nAnd I don’t think anyone can hear my thoughts. That sounds… more extreme than what I’ve been dealing with, I guess. I’m answering that right, aren’t I? Like, I’d know if that was happening?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other strange sounds, voices, or sensations that other people around you didn’t seem to notice, even briefly, at any time in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s been anything else, really… not that I can remember from before.\n\nIt’s just this thing at work, and I know I keep coming back to it. During the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, I’ll hear voices coming through the monitor speakers, even when every channel is muted. That’s just the part that scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time where you felt persistently sad, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy, for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days lately it feels like this heavy gray mood is already there before I even get out of bed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be driving in to my shift and just seeing the casino lights can make me tear up, even if nothing really happened. It feels silly saying that out loud, but… yes, it’s been more than a couple weeks, I think. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, or not getting as much pleasure from them as before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I mean, I still tell myself I’m just tired from work, but it’s not really the same.\n\nI used to really look forward to salsa class, and softball, and I’d get excited about building PCs or having people over for board games. Lately I just… ignore the texts. I stay home and sit there like none of it can really reach me. That counts, right? Like, that’s what you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt your thoughts were racing, where it was different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had nights where I don’t sleep well, especially with work, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually energetic from it or like I didn’t need sleep.\n\nMy thoughts can feel busy when I’m worried, but not in that… sped-up way, I don’t think. If that makes sense? I don’t think I’ve had a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others around you didn’t share, even when there was evidence against it, like believing someone was following you or that you had a special ability?\n\nPatient: I… not like special abilities, no. And I don’t think anyone is following me exactly.\n\nBut there is something I get kind of stuck on at work. I keep replaying every missed camera angle or any call I made a little late, like it’s proof I’m careless… dangerous, even. Other people tell me I did what I could or that it wasn’t that serious, but it doesn’t really land.\n\nPart of me feels like if something goes wrong on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I know that probably sounds extreme. I’m not trying to be dramatic… I just don’t know how else to explain it. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something very strongly that most people would think is not true, like thinking you are being watched by unseen forces or that you have a special role or mission that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not unseen forces or a special mission, no… I don’t think it’s like that.\n\nIt’s more… I get really convinced I’ve messed up at work, even if people tell me I didn’t. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days lately it feels like this heavy gray mood is already there before I even get out of bed.\n\nSometimes I’ll be driving in to my shift and just seeing the casino lights can make me tear up, even if nothing really happened. It feels silly saying that out loud, but… yes, it’s been more than a couple weeks, I think. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, or not getting as much pleasure from them as before?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I mean, I still tell myself I’m just tired from work, but it’s not really the same.\n\nI used to really look forward to salsa class, and softball, and I’d get excited about building PCs or having people over for board games. Lately I just… ignore the texts. I stay home and sit there like none of it can really reach me. That counts, right? Like, that’s what you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt your thoughts were racing, where it was different from your usual self?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had nights where I don’t sleep well, especially with work, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually energetic from it or like I didn’t need sleep.\n\nMy thoughts can feel busy when I’m worried, but not in that… sped-up way, I don’t think. If that makes sense? I don’t think I’ve had a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others around you didn’t share, even when there was evidence against it, like believing someone was following you or that you had a special ability?\n\nPatient: I… not like special abilities, no. And I don’t think anyone is following me exactly.\n\nBut there is something I get kind of stuck on at work. I keep replaying every missed camera angle or any call I made a little late, like it’s proof I’m careless… dangerous, even. Other people tell me I did what I could or that it wasn’t that serious, but it doesn’t really land.\n\nPart of me feels like if something goes wrong on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I know that probably sounds extreme. I’m not trying to be dramatic… I just don’t know how else to explain it. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something very strongly that most people would think is not true, like thinking you are being watched by unseen forces or that you have a special role or mission that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not unseen forces or a special mission, no… I don’t think it’s like that.\n\nIt’s more… I get really convinced I’ve messed up at work, even if people tell me I didn’t. Like I’ll replay a missed camera angle or a call I made too late, and it feels like proof that I’m careless, or dangerous somehow. And if something goes wrong on my watch, part of me feels like… I deserve to be punished for it.\n\nI know that probably sounds too harsh. I’m not trying to make it into something it isn’t. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something strongly that wasn’t true, even when people gave you proof it wasn’t, like thinking someone was lying to you or that something bad was going to happen that didn’t seem real to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve thought someone was lying to me, exactly. It’s more about work… and I know I’ve kind of said this already, sorry.\n\nI’ll get really fixed on a missed camera angle or a call I made later than I should have, and it starts to feel like definite proof that I’m careless, or that I’m dangerous to have in that role. Even if someone shows me it wasn’t my fault or says I did what I could, I have a hard time believing them.\n\nAnd then I think… if something bad happens on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I know that probably sounds excessive. I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something very strongly that wasn’t true and couldn’t be convinced otherwise, even when others gave you clear evidence it wasn’t real—like thinking something bad was going to happen that they didn’t believe, or that you had a special role no one else understood?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had a special role or anything like that. And I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut… yes, I guess there are times I get very convinced I’ve done something wrong at work. I’ll keep going over a missed camera angle or a call I made too late, and it starts to feel like clear proof that I’m careless, like I’m dangerous to have there. People can tell me it wasn’t my fault or that I handled it okay, and I hear them, but I don’t really believe it.\n\nAnd then I get this thought that if anything goes wrong on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I know that probably sounds extreme… I’m sorry. Is that what you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, have you ever had beliefs or thoughts that others around you didn’t share, and that you held onto even when there was evidence that they weren’t true?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… not really, I don’t think. I mean, I don’t have beliefs about other people or anything unusual like that.\n\nIt mostly stays tied to work, if that makes sense. I keep replaying things I missed, like a camera angle I didn’t catch or a call I made late, and it feels like proof that I’m careless… or dangerous to have in that position. And even when people try to reassure me, part of me still thinks if something goes wrong while I’m there, I deserve to be punished for it.\n\nI know that may not exactly answer the “outside of work” part. I’m sorry—I’m trying to be clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any delusions or hallucinations outside of the voices you’ve heard at work—like seeing things others don’t, believing something strongly that isn’t true, or hearing voices when no one is around?\n\nPatient: Not really seeing things, no. And I don’t think I hear them other places… or at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nThe thing that scared me enough to come in is at work, during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room. I’ll hear voices through the monitor speakers saying things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” even when I’m pretty sure every channel is muted. And no one else hears it.\n\nI know that sounds… strange. I’m not trying to make it dramatic. That’s just the part I couldn’t explain away anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any delusions or other types of hallucinations—like seeing things that aren’t there or strongly believing something that others don’t share—outside of the voices you’ve heard at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had other kinds, like seeing things. And the strong beliefs… I mean, it’s mostly tied to that same fear at work, not something separate.\n\nWhat scared me enough to come in is really the voices in the surveillance room. During quiet stretches, I’ll hear them through the monitor speakers saying things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” even when I’m pretty sure every channel is muted. And no one else hears anything.\n\nI know I’ve said that already. I’m sorry—I just want to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed something strongly that wasn’t true or heard voices when no one was around, even briefly, before this started happening at work?\n\nPatient: No… not before this. At least, not in the way it’s been happening now.\n\nBefore this whole stretch—after the surveillance lead changed and I started feeling so… hopeless and guilty—I never heard accusations coming through equipment or anything like that. And I didn’t have this certainty that I was dangerous to everyone, or that something awful would happen because of me.\n\nIt seems tied to this same low period. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. When I’m not at work… no, I don’t think so. I haven’t seen things or felt anything like that that other people didn’t.

I haven’t felt like anyone is putting thoughts in my head or controlling them.\n\nAnd I don’t think anyone can hear my thoughts. That sounds… more extreme than what I’ve been dealing with, I guess. I’m answering that right, aren’t I? During the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, I’ll hear voices coming through the monitor speakers, even when every channel is muted. That’s just the part that scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time where you felt persistently sad, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy, for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I’ve had nights where I don’t sleep well, especially with work, but I wouldn’t say I felt unusually energetic from it or like I didn’t need sleep.\n\nMy thoughts can feel busy when I’m worried, but not in that… sped-up way, I don’t think. If that makes sense? I don’t think I’ve had a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others around you didn’t share, even when there was evidence against it, like believing someone was following you or that you had a special ability?\n\nPatient: I… not like special abilities, no. And I don’t think anyone is following me exactly.\n\nBut there is something I get kind of stuck on at work. I keep replaying every missed camera angle or any call I made a little late, like it’s proof I’m careless… dangerous, even. Other people tell me I did what I could or that it wasn’t that serious, but it doesn’t really land.\n\nPart of me feels like if something goes wrong on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I know that probably sounds extreme. I’m not trying to be dramatic… I just don’t know how else to explain it. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something very strongly that most people would think is not true, like thinking you are being watched by unseen forces or that you have a special role or mission that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not unseen forces or a special mission, no… I don’t think it’s like that.\n\nIt’s more… I get really convinced I’ve messed up at work, even if people tell me I didn’t. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something strongly that wasn’t true, even when people gave you proof it wasn’t, like thinking someone was lying to you or that something bad was going to happen that didn’t seem real to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve thought someone was lying to me, exactly. Even if someone shows me it wasn’t my fault or says I did what I could, I have a hard time believing them.\n\nAnd then I think… if something bad happens on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it. I keep replaying things I missed, like a camera angle I didn’t catch or a call I made late, and it feels like proof that I’m careless… or dangerous to have in that position. And even when people try to reassure me, part of me still thinks if something goes wrong while I’m there, I deserve to be punished for it.\n\nI know that may not exactly answer the “outside of work” part. And I don’t think I hear them other places… or at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nThe thing that scared me enough to come in is at work, during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room. I’ll hear voices through the monitor speakers saying things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” even when I’m pretty sure every channel is muted. And no one else hears it.\n\nI know that sounds… strange. I’m not trying to make it dramatic. That’s just the part I couldn’t explain away anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any delusions or other types of hallucinations—like seeing things that aren’t there or strongly believing something that others don’t share—outside of the voices you’ve heard at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had other kinds, like seeing things. And the strong beliefs… I mean, it’s mostly tied to that same fear at work, not something separate.\n\nWhat scared me enough to come in is really the voices in the surveillance room. And no one else hears anything.\n\nI know I’ve said that already. I’m sorry—I just want to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed something strongly that wasn’t true or heard voices when no one was around, even briefly, before this started happening at work?\n\nPatient: No… not before this. At least, not in the way it’s been happening now.\n\nBefore this whole stretch—after the surveillance lead changed and I started feeling so… hopeless and guilty—I never heard accusations coming through equipment or anything like that. And I didn’t have this certainty that I was dangerous to everyone, or that something awful would happen because of me.\n\nIt seems tied to this same low period. I hope that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences where you saw, heard, or felt something that others around you didn’t, even briefly, when you weren’t at work?\n\nPatient: When I’m not at work… no, I don’t think so.
